  - Money is `Decimal(38, 12)` (gross, net, unit price, mechanical fee), share rate `Decimal(18, 8)`: exact strings from the file, sums do not overflow (all-time net is about 1.6e19 pico, above Int64). No `_pico` columns.
  - Equal keys are NOT merged in storage. `SummingMergeTree` was rejected: it cannot keep unit price and names as in the report, merges are asynchronous so reads need `sum() GROUP BY` anyway, and rows summing to zero are dropped.
  - Equal keys are summed by views (06 decision 11): `lines` (per file and key), `units` (ISRC + UPC + report month + sales month, per file; replaces `source_units`, D-088, once measured), `breakdown(isrc)`, `file_totals`, `pair_attrs`, `pair_attrs_latest`, `pairs`. Views carry `file_id`; the account comes from the registry.
  - Partition is `(toYYYYMM(reporting_month), file_id)`: one partition per file. A load goes through `believe_stage.rows_<file>`, then `ATTACH PARTITION ... FROM` (new file) or `REPLACE PARTITION` (changed file, 06 question 5); deleting a file is `DROP PARTITION`. Checked on synthetic rows against ClickHouse 26.8.
  - Checked on the real July 2026 report (3,487,382 lines, 2,345,182 keys, as in 06): within one key there is never more than one label, artist, track, release or catalog number; 317,764 keys have several unit prices. Other months and the second account are not checked yet. All 46 zipped reports have the same 21-column header.
  - Not measured yet: size and view speed on the full history. A projection on the unit grain is the planned fix if `units` is slow.
- References: 06 decisions 2, 3, 11; D-010, D-088; `migrations/clickhouse/0001_believe.sql`
- Status: accepted (design); needs a measurement on the full history before D-088 is retired

## D-103: Docker Compose, ClickHouse migrations (supersedes "do not use Docker" in CLAUDE.md for this work)
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: how to run PostgreSQL and ClickHouse and how to version the ClickHouse schema.
- Decision:
  - `docker-compose.yml` (`postgres:18.6`, `clickhouse/clickhouse-server:26.8`, one-shot `clickhouse-migrate`), `docker-compose.dev.yml` (ports on 127.0.0.1, named volumes), `.env` (git-ignored) and `.env.example`. Start with `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.dev.yml up -d`. Do not use `--wait` for the whole stack: it reports the finished one-shot service as an error.
  - ClickHouse config is mounted as the single file `config/clickhouse/config.d/ops.xml`, never the whole `config.d`: the image keeps `docker_related_config.xml` there, and hiding it makes the server listen only inside the container.
  - Migrations are `migrations/clickhouse/NNNN_name.sql`, applied by `scripts/migrate-clickhouse.ts` (no dependencies, plain HTTP, runs on Node with type stripping, so no constructor parameter properties). Applied versions are kept in `<db>.schema_migrations` with a sha256: an edited applied file is an error, a failed migration is not recorded and runs again, so every statement must be idempotent. Ten unit tests in `tests/clickhouse-migrate.test.ts`.
  - The runner waits for the server, because the image restarts it once on the first start. No lock: it must run as the single one-shot service. PostgreSQL migrations are not set up.
  - The log level is `information`, console only; the container log is capped at 10 MB x 3. No backups for now (owner's decision); ClickHouse data can be rebuilt from the stored CSV files.
- References: D-011, D-102; `docker-compose.yml`, `scripts/migrate-clickhouse.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-104: File registry in PostgreSQL, load order across PostgreSQL and ClickHouse
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: where the file id, sha256 and account live once the lines are in ClickHouse, and how a load stays consistent across two stores.
- Decision:
  - Table `source_files` in PostgreSQL (`migrations/postgres/0001_source_files.sql`): id (= `file_id` in ClickHouse), source, account, reporting_month (first day), report_id, file_name, stored_path, sha256, status, line_count, net_total, gross_total, uploaded_by, created_at (= upload time), updated_at (trigger). Dropped from the SQLite table: generated_at, generated_at_source, warnings, key_count (owner's decision; warnings such as "lines without ISRC" are computed from the data).
  - Statuses: `loading`, `loaded`, `failed`, `superseded` (a new file for the same account and month replaces the old one). Unique indexes (one file per source + account + month, one per sha256) apply only to `loading` and `loaded`, so a failed or replaced file never blocks a new one. `loaded` requires the control numbers.
  - sha256 is the hash of the file exactly as uploaded (an archive is hashed as an archive) and is computed before the registry row is created. Same CSV in another archive is caught by the scope index, not by sha256.
  - Account and month are first taken from the file name, then the month is checked against the "Reporting month" column of every row. The account cannot be checked against the content.
  - Load order: sha256, then `loading` row, then parse and validate while streaming into `believe_stage.rows_<id>` (no summing: ClickHouse views sum), then compare the stage with the control numbers, then `ATTACH PARTITION`, then `loaded` with the totals. If writing `loaded` fails, drop the partition and write `failed`.
  - Control numbers (line count, gross, net) are summed by the loader with decimal.js, with no rounding, from the CSV lines, never read back from ClickHouse.
  - Readers ask ClickHouse only for `file_id IN (files with status loaded)`: the short window between ATTACH and `loaded`, and leftovers of failed loads, never reach a calculation. To be built in the sources module.
  - PostgreSQL is assumed always available: no recovery sweep for rows left in `loading`.
  - Not done yet: the PostgreSQL migration runner, one schema per module (postponed by the owner), the loader itself.
- References: D-102, D-103, 06 decisions 8 and 11, open question 5; `migrations/postgres/0001_source_files.sql`
- Status: accepted

## D-105: PostgreSQL migration runner as a one-shot compose service
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: how PostgreSQL migrations run next to the ClickHouse ones (D-103).
- Decision:
  - `scripts/migrate-postgres.sh` runs in the `postgres:18.6` image (it has psql) as the compose service `postgres-migrate`: waits for the server, applies `migrations/postgres/NNNN_name.sql` in order and exits. Run by hand: `npm run migrate:pg` (optionally `-- --status`).
  - Every migration and its `schema_migrations` row (version, name, sha256) are one transaction (`psql -1`): a failing migration leaves nothing behind. An edited applied file, a badly named file, a repeated version or a new file below an applied version stops the run. Checked on the real stack, including the half-applied migration case and a cold start from empty volumes.
  - The ClickHouse runner stays separate (D-103): its DDL is not transactional, so it records a migration only after all statements ran.
  - No lock: one one-shot service per stack. No automated tests for the shell script, only the manual scenarios above.
- References: D-103, D-104; `scripts/migrate-postgres.sh`, `docker-compose.yml`
- Status: accepted

## D-106: pgAdmin in the dev stack
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: a web UI to look into PostgreSQL during development.
- Decision: `dpage/pgadmin4:9.19` in `docker-compose.dev.yml` only (not in the base file), on `127.0.0.1:${PGADMIN_PORT}` (5050). Desktop mode (`PGADMIN_CONFIG_SERVER_MODE=False`), so no login page; `PGADMIN_EMAIL` and `PGADMIN_PASSWORD` stay in `.env` as the required bootstrap values. The server is pre-registered from `config/pgadmin/servers.json` (host `postgres`, database and user `erp`: edit the file if `.env` changes them); the database password is asked once. State lives in the named volume `pgadmin_data`. pgAdmin is PostgreSQL only: ClickHouse is viewed in its Play page (`/play`) or a client such as DBeaver.
- References: D-103; `docker-compose.dev.yml`
- Status: accepted

## D-107: Database libraries: pg + Drizzle, @clickhouse/client
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: which libraries talk to PostgreSQL and ClickHouse.
- Decision:
  - PostgreSQL: `pg` 8 with `drizzle-orm/node-postgres` (Drizzle is already the project standard). Migrations stay our SQL files (D-103, D-105), no drizzle-kit. `src/shared/postgres.ts` (`createPostgres`, config from `DB_*`): per-pool type parsers, `date` as 'YYYY-MM-DD' text and `timestamptz` as ISO UTC text (pg would build a Date in the local zone); `numeric` and `bigint` stay exact text.
  - ClickHouse: official `@clickhouse/client` over HTTP. `src/shared/clickhouse.ts` (`createClickHouse`, config from `CLICKHOUSE_*`) forces `output_format_json_quote_decimals=1` and `output_format_json_quote_64bit_integers=1`: the server writes Decimal and Int64/Int128 as JSON numbers by default and JavaScript rounds them (9007199254740993 becomes ...992). Corrects the earlier assumption that they arrive as strings. Request and response compression on, `send_progress_in_http_headers` on for long inserts.
  - Money from either database is read as text and goes to decimal.js from `shared/money.ts` only: plain decimal.js keeps 20 digits and silently rounds a 21-digit sum; the application sets 40.
  - Integration tests (`tests/postgres.test.ts`, `tests/clickhouse.test.ts`) need the compose stack. Without it they are skipped with a warning; `REQUIRE_STACK=1` makes the skip a failure.
  - `Number(env.DB_PORT ...)`-style connection settings are added to the allowlist of the "no floats in money" test (settings, not money).
- References: D-011, D-102, D-103; `src/shared/postgres.ts`, `src/shared/clickhouse.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-108: Believe loader for ClickHouse and the PostgreSQL registry
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: how a report goes into believe.rows and source_files (D-102, D-104).
- Decision:
  - `createBelieveLoader(...).ingestBelieve({path, fileName, uploadedBy})` in `src/modules/sources/loader.ts` is the single entry point. `scripts/load-believe.ts` is a thin loop around it; the API will call the same function once reads move to ClickHouse. Today the API still calls the old SQLite loader, so two loaders exist until then.
  - Order: sha256, duplicate checks (409 `duplicate_period`, `duplicate_file`), copy to the storage dir, `loading` row, stage table `believe_stage.rows_<id>`, streamed lines in batches of 50,000 with one insert in flight, control check, `ATTACH PARTITION`, `loaded` with totals. The control check compares line count, gross and net (decimal.js from `shared/money.ts`) read from the file with the stage table, and requires exactly one reporting month, the one in the file name.
  - Validation per line as before: field count, month equals the file month, quantity is an integer, amounts have at most 12 decimals (share rate 8), currency USD. An empty currency is stored as USD (as the SQLite loader did; the warning is gone). An optional column missing from the header is stored as 0 for amounts and rates.
  - Any failure drops the stage table, drops the partition if it was attached, and marks the row `failed`. The stored copy of the file is kept for a failed load (the SQLite loader deleted it). A failed row never blocks the month: a corrected file is accepted.
  - Registry totals are shown without padding zeros (`0.595`, not `0.595000000000`).
  - Real July 2026 report (account 1052428): 3,487,382 lines, 2,345,182 keys and net 790209.904499203132, the same figures as the reconciliation with the old ERP (D-067); loaded in 36.8 s, 657 MB RSS at the end (peak 883 MB), 75 MiB on disk for 332 MiB uncompressed; the `units` view over this month takes 0.06 s, `breakdown` for one ISRC 0.01 s.
  - Not done: reading (units, breakdown, active files, pair attributes) from ClickHouse, switching the API, the other 46 files, removing the SQLite sources code and `source_units` (D-088).
- References: D-102, D-104, D-107; `src/modules/sources/loader.ts`, `registry.ts`, `columnar.ts`, `tests/sources-loader.test.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-109: Sources service on PostgreSQL + ClickHouse (step 1 of leaving SQLite)
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: the SQLite dependency of the Believe module goes away step by step; the facade must see the same outputs.
- Decision:
  - `createColumnarSourcesService({registry, columnar, storageDir})` (`src/modules/sources/columnar-service.ts`) has the methods of the SQLite service, all async: `ingestBelieve`, `listFiles`, `getFile`, `activeFiles`, `units(filter)`, `breakdown(isrc)`, `pairAttributes()`. `SourceUnit` is unchanged (`account`, `isrc`, `upc`, months `YYYY-MM`, `netPico` as bigint), so `calculate()` needs no change except `await`. File records have the registry fields (D-104): no `generatedAt`, `keyCount`, `warnings`; `lineCount` and totals can be null while a file is loading or failed; `uploadedAt` is now `createdAt`.
  - Every read first takes the `loaded` files from the registry and asks ClickHouse only for them (`file_id IN`), so a loading, failed or replaced file never reaches a calculation. `units` reads the `units` view; `breakdown` and the pair names read the rows directly with the same file filter, because their views have no file parameter.
  - The account of a unit comes from the registry by file id (the rows carry only `file_id`).
  - Pair names: from the latest reporting month in which the pair appears.
  - `createBelieveColumnar` takes the database names, so a test can use its own databases. The ClickHouse trap met twice: a result alias equal to a column name (`toString(file_id) AS file_id`) shadows the column in WHERE. Result aliases now differ from column names.
  - Tests of the new code run in a private PostgreSQL database and private ClickHouse databases with the real migrations applied (`tests/helpers/sources-stack.ts`), dropped afterwards; the development data is not touched.
  - Not done: the facade and the API still use the SQLite service; `calculate()`, `reconciliation`, `ownerBalance`, `signals`, `explainTrack`, `createPayout` are still synchronous; the old SQLite sources code and `source_units` (D-088) are still there.
- References: D-102, D-104, D-108; `src/modules/sources/columnar-service.ts`, `tests/sources-service.test.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-110: The facade and the API run on the new sources; the SQLite sources code is removed
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: step 2 of leaving SQLite in the Believe module (D-109): switch the callers and delete the old code.
- Decision:
  - `openFacade({ dataDir, payoutThresholdCents, sources })` takes the sources service (`createSourcesService`, `src/modules/sources/service.ts`; it is the former `columnar-service`). The caller opens and closes the connections: `src/connect-sources.ts` (settings from `.env`) is used by `main.ts` and the scripts. The sources module now has `registry.ts`, `columnar.ts`, `loader.ts`, `service.ts`, `believe-format.ts`, `csv.ts`; `schema.ts`, `repository.ts`, the SQLite service, `openSourcesModule`, `source_lines`, `source_units`, `attr_sets`, `parseBelieveRow` and `lineKey` are gone (D-088 retired).
  - `calculate()` is async and reads `sources.snapshot()` first (the files in force and their units, together), then the other modules in one synchronous stretch. `ownerBalance`, `reconciliation`, `signals`, `explainTrack` and `createPayout` are async; the API handlers await them. A payout snapshot records the Believe files of the very calculation it used (`calc.believeFiles`), not a later read of the registry; the reconciliation compares the file totals only for the files of its own calculation.
  - API: `POST /api/sources/believe` (multipart, field `file`, optional `fileName` to send a file under its real name) answers 201 with the registry record, 400 for bad data with the line number, 409 `duplicate_period` / `duplicate_file`. `GET /api/sources/files` lists every registry record including `failed` and `superseded`. `generatedAt` is no longer accepted for Believe (B17 keeps it). The sources page shows the new fields.
  - Tests: the facade tests get their sources from `tests/helpers/test-sources.ts`: one private pair of PostgreSQL and ClickHouse databases per test file, created on first use, emptied before each new facade, dropped after the file. The facade tests therefore need the compose stack (they fail with a clear message without it). About 90 call sites got `await`; `expect(() => f.createPayout()).toThrow()` became `await expect(f.createPayout()).rejects.toThrow()`.
  - Tests dropped on purpose: recovery of a load interrupted by a crash (a row left in `loading` now blocks its period and sha256 until it is removed by hand; PostgreSQL is assumed to be always available) and the `source_units` backfill. Tests of the SQLite internals (`source_lines` rows, `merged_lines`) now read the ClickHouse `lines` view.
  - Believe files are no longer part of the demo SQLite data: `data/demo` and `data/real` have no `sources.sqlite`; the reports are loaded through the API into PostgreSQL and ClickHouse (`data/dev` is used here for the local SQL files and stored originals).
  - README, CLAUDE.md and HANDOVER.md say that Docker is now required (the earlier "no Docker" rule was lifted by the owner).
- References: D-102 to D-109; `src/facade/index.ts`, `src/facade/calculation.ts`, `src/api/app.ts`, `src/connect-sources.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-111: The whole Believe history loaded through the API; measurements
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: does the new storage reproduce the history of the SQLite sources, and how fast is it?
- Decision (all numbers measured on this machine, the dev stack in Docker):
  - 46 archives and one plain CSV from `ERP_data/believe` and `believe_echo8` were sent one by one to `POST /api/sources/believe`: 44 through a shell loop (the two already loaded months answered 409 `duplicate_period`), the two archives named like `... .csv (1).zip` / `(2).zip` separately, the July CSV of the second account under its real name through the `fileName` field. All 47 files loaded, none failed; about 11 minutes for 44 files, 12 to 37 s for a file of 1 to 3.6 million lines. Not loaded on purpose: the unpacked copies of December 2023 in `believe/raw` and `believebackstage-2026-08-05 (1).csv`.
  - Same totals as the old SQLite registry: account 1052428 34 files, 62,066,587 lines, net 15,287,531; account 1055956 13 files, 13,678,165 lines, net 1,050,945. The `units` view has 721,975 rows and 20,686 ISRC, the same as the old `source_units`. The reconciliation over 34 months shows no difference between the calculation and the totals stored at load; July 2026 is 890,683.17 (790,209.90 + 100,473.27).
  - Storage: 75.74 million rows, 47 partitions, 1.61 GiB on disk (7.06 GiB uncompressed) against 27 GB in SQLite.
  - Speed over the whole history: `units` view 1.7 s; `GET /api/reconciliation` 4.3 s; `GET /api/signals` 6.0 s; monthly net by `GROUP BY` 0.3 s. The `lines` view (every key of 48 million) takes 26 s: it is for checks, not for the calculation. D-088 asked for the aggregate because the same read took 490 s in SQLite.
  - Open: the two-account Believe data has no owners or splits in `data/dev`, so everything shows as unallocated until module 1 is filled (`npm run import:legacy` needs the stack too).
- References: D-108, D-110; `ERP_data/`
- Status: accepted

## D-112: Scope and accepted limits of the Believe move (owner's decisions after D-111)
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: what counts as done for the move of the Believe module to PostgreSQL and ClickHouse, and what is left open on purpose.
- Decision:
  - **Scope:** only the Believe module and what touches it in the facade. Publishing (B17) stays on SQLite and is not reviewed or reconciled in this move.
  - **Demo:** there is no demo mode any more. The demo data of the handover package has no Believe data, and anything that needs the sources needs the compose stack. The demo sections of HANDOVER.md and `npm run demo` are outdated and are not maintained.
  - **Replacing or deleting a file:** technically possible in the storage (`superseded` status, `DROP PARTITION` and `ATTACH PARTITION` in `columnar.ts`), but there is no API and no UI for it, and it is not required now. A different file for an account and month that already has a loaded file is refused with `409 duplicate_period`, naming the loaded file and the first 12 characters of its sha256 (the new file's sha256 is not in the message; it may be added to the error details later). The same file again is `409 duplicate_file`. So a changed report for an existing period is seen as an error, not replaced silently (06, open question 5 stays open).
  - **Load failures:** an error of the parser, a validation, ClickHouse or the control check is caught: the stage table is dropped, an attached partition is dropped, the row becomes `failed`, the period is free again. Only a crash of the process (killed server, power loss) or an unreachable PostgreSQL when `loaded` or `failed` is written leaves a row in `loading`; it blocks its account, month and sha256 and is removed by hand: `UPDATE source_files SET status = 'failed' WHERE id = '<id>'`, then `DROP TABLE believe_stage.rows_<id without dashes>` and, if the partition was attached, `ALTER TABLE believe.rows DROP PARTITION (<YYYYMM>, '<id>')`. A copy of the file stays in the storage directory after any failure.
  - **Postponed:** deployment of the application itself (no Dockerfile yet), backups (decided earlier), and the memory of the server during a calculation (every request reads all units into memory; not measured).
- References: D-103, D-108, D-110, D-111; 06 open question 5
- Status: accepted

## D-113: Lines page of the Believe reports (`/sources/lines`)
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: a page to look at the Believe data: choose report files as a filter, filter the lines, from the `lines` view and not from the bare `rows`.
- Decision:
  - API `GET /api/sources/lines`: query `fileId` (comma-separated ids of loaded files), `isrc` (any spelling), `upc`, `platform`, `country`, `subscriptionType` (`(none)` is the empty one), `salesType`, `releaseType`, `salesFrom`/`salesTo` (YYYY-MM), `sort` (`net`, `gross`, `quantity`, `reportingMonth`, `salesMonth`, `isrc`), `dir`, `limit` (1 to 1000, default 100), `offset`. Answer: `files` (those read), `items` (one record per key of a file, equal keys summed, with `account`, `mergedLines`, and the artist and track of the pair from its latest report month), `total` (keys, quantity, gross, net and report lines of everything that matches, not only the page), `limit`, `offset`, `sort`, `dir`. `GET /api/sources/lines/facets?fileId=` gives the values of the drop-downs. Counts and money are exact text.
  - Files: only loaded files are accepted (400 otherwise). Without `fileId`: all loaded files if an ISRC or UPC is given, else the files of the newest report month, because the view over all history takes about 20 s (measured: one file about 2 s, one ISRC over all 47 files 0.05 to 0.1 s, all 48 million keys 23 s). The page and the totals are two scans run side by side. Every filter value is a query parameter; the sort name is mapped to a column, nothing of the request becomes SQL text.
  - UI: the Sources section has two tabs, "Files and checks" (the former page) and "Lines of the reports": a list of the loaded files with check boxes (newest month, all, none), filters, sort, page size and a pager. The first visit chooses the files of the newest month. The totals line shows the count of keys and report lines, quantity, gross and net. Typing an ISRC with files chosen searches those files; with none chosen, all files.
  - Tests: 7 service tests (default, summing and names, ISRC spelling, filters, sort and paging, refusals, drop-down values) and one API test. The slow pages (calculation, 5 to 6 s) are not changed.
- References: D-102, D-109, D-111; `src/modules/sources/{columnar,service}.ts`, `client/src/sources/SourceLinesPage.tsx`
- Status: accepted

## D-114: The move of the Believe module is closed
- Date: 2026-10-31
- Question: is the Believe module on a proper database, so that the question of the move can be closed?
- Decision: yes, closed by the owner. Done and checked: loading through the API into PostgreSQL (registry) and ClickHouse (lines), all 47 files with the same totals as the old SQLite registry (D-111); reading, calculation, reconciliation of July 2026 against the old ERP (the same numbers and the same 98 explained differences), the owner balance and the payout button on real figures (owner "Hwungii": 278,397.626110924207894 earned, a payout of 27,839,762 cents rounded down, the snapshot lists the 47 files with their sha256, a second payout is blocked until a payment proof), the Sources pages including the lines page (D-113), the removal of the SQLite sources code (D-110). 182 tests, typecheck and lint pass.
- Left open on purpose (D-112): the API and UI to replace or delete a file, hand removal of a row left in `loading` by a crash, deployment of the application, backups, the memory of the server during a calculation, the 5 to 6 s of the pages that recalculate and the slow list of splits (to be optimised later, as separate tasks), the demo mode. Publishing (B17) is the next module.
- References: D-102 to D-113
- Status: accepted

## D-115: Repository layout: one repo, documents in `docs/`, reference data in `context_data/`
- Date: 2026-11-01
- Question: how to put the code and the architecture documents into git, and where to keep the reference data that must not be committed?
- Decision: the repository root is `ERP/`. The former `новая_архитектура/app/` content is the root itself; the former `новая_архитектура/` (documents, `аудит/`, `решения/`, this log) is `docs/`. The old handover folders `ERP_data/`, `data/` and `context/` live in `context_data/`, which is git-ignored except its `README.md` (it describes what is inside and the rules). `/data/` (application databases) is git-ignored too. `context_data/` and `data/` are in `.dockerignore`.
- Consequences: the paths `../../data/...` became `context_data/data/...`; Believe, splits and publishing sources are in `context_data/ERP_data/{believe,splits,publishing}`; `app/<x>` in the documents became `<x>`. Older entries of this log keep their original wording where they describe the archives of 2026-10-31 (`новая_архитектура/`, `../../data/`).
- References: `.gitignore`, `.dockerignore`, `context_data/README.md`, `scripts/{import-legacy,reconcile-erp,add-writer-aliases,restore-publishing-terms}.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-116: Monorepo with npm workspaces: `packages/{backend,frontend,shared}`
- Date: 2026-11-01
- Question: the backend and the UI shared one `package.json`; should they be separate packages?
- Decision: yes. The root `package.json` only declares `workspaces: ["packages/*"]`, the dev tooling shared by all (typescript, eslint, prettier) and scripts that delegate to the workspaces. `packages/backend` (`@erp/backend`: `src`, `tests`, `scripts`, `migrations`, `init`, `config`, its own `tsconfig` and `vitest.config`), `packages/frontend` (`@erp/frontend`: the former `client/`), `packages/shared` (`@erp/shared`: `api-schemas.ts`, depends on zod only, imported as `@erp/shared/api-schemas`, TypeScript is consumed without a build). The rest of the former `src/shared` (money, dates, db, ...) stays in the backend because the browser does not use it. `data/`, `context_data/`, `docs/`, `.env` and the docker-compose files stay in the repository root; the compose files mount `packages/backend/{init,config,migrations,scripts}`. One `npm ci` in the root, one `package-lock.json`.
- Consequences: the code finds the root with `import.meta.dirname` (not `process.cwd()`), so tests and scripts work from any directory; the default data directory is still `<root>/data`, `.env` is read from the root, the client build is `packages/frontend/dist`. `npm run demo` passes `../../data/demo`. ESLint now also checks the frontend (a stale `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` disable comment was removed because that plugin is not installed). Older entries keep their old paths (`src/...`, `client/...`, `scripts/...`): read them as under `packages/backend/` or `packages/frontend/`.
- Checked: typecheck of the three workspaces, eslint, `vite build`, 182 backend tests with `REQUIRE_STACK=1`.
- References: `package.json`, `packages/*/package.json`, `docker-compose.yml`, D-115
- Status: accepted

## D-117: `repository.ts` for settlements and publishing, facade split by topic, private test scratch folders
- Date: 2026-11-01
- Question: the rule "all SQL lives in the repository" was broken by settlements and publishing (SQL inside `service.ts`); `facade/index.ts` had 650 lines; `data/test-tmp` grew to 919 MB with 2112 leftover folders.
- Decision: `modules/settlements/repository.ts` and `modules/publishing/repository.ts` now hold all the SQL (Drizzle and the prepared statements); the services keep only the rules and call the repository. Behaviour is unchanged. `facade/index.ts` only opens the modules and merges the groups of methods: `context.ts` (shared checks and the modules), `splits.ts`, `sources.ts`, `reports.ts` (reconciliation, balance, signals, track explanation), `owner-view.ts`, `payouts.ts` (operations, the payout button, payment proofs, returns). The public `Facade` type is the same. `modules/owners/service.ts` (626 lines) is left whole on purpose: its parts share helpers and call each other. Every test file now writes under its own `data/test-tmp/run-*` folder (`tests/helpers/tmp-root.ts`) that `tests/setup.ts` removes after the file.
- Consequences: publishing's control count is compared as `bigint` so that no `Number(...)` appears outside the allowed places (rules-coverage test).
- Checked: typecheck, eslint, 182 tests; `data/test-tmp` emptied by hand once.
- References: D-116
- Status: accepted

## D-118: API routes split into routers, JSON API versioned as `/api/v1`
- Date: 2026-11-01
- Question: `api/app.ts` held about 50 routes in one function; how to structure it so that a new module adds its own file, and how to leave room for incompatible changes?
- Decision: `api/app.ts` only assembles the app (JSON parser, `/api/v1`, the 404 for unknown API routes, the built client, the error handler). Routes live in `api/routes/{owners,splits,sources,publishing,reports,settlements}.ts`, each exporting `xRouter(facade, opts?)`; `routes/index.ts` (`createV1Router`) joins them and adds `/health`. `api/http.ts` holds `param`, `parse` and the central error handler. Paths are unchanged apart from the prefix: `/api/...` became `/api/v1/...`; the old unversioned paths answer 404. The frontend client (`/api/v1`) and `HANDOVER.md` were updated.
- Consequences: a new module adds a router file and one line in `routes/index.ts`. A breaking change goes to `/api/v2` next to v1. The routes still only validate input and call the facade or one module service. Request schemas stay in `@erp/shared/api-schemas`. Response schemas and the OpenAPI description are not part of this change.
- Checked: typecheck, eslint, 182 tests.
- References: D-117
- Status: accepted

## D-119: API contract as data: response schemas in `shared`, OpenAPI at `/api/v1/openapi.json`, Swagger UI at `/api/v1/docs`
- Date: 2026-11-01
- Question: how to document the JSON API for people and AI agents without a second hand-written description that drifts from the code?
- Decision: `packages/shared/src/api-responses.ts` holds the zod schemas of the response bodies (next to the request schemas in `api-schemas.ts`). `packages/backend/src/api/operations.ts` lists every operation of `/api/v1` (method, path, tag, request schemas, response schema, success status, known errors). `api/openapi.ts` builds the OpenAPI 3.1 document from that list with `@asteasolutions/zod-to-openapi`; `routes/index.ts` serves it at `/api/v1/openapi.json` and the Swagger UI (`swagger-ui-express`) at `/api/v1/docs`. The frontend client takes its response types (`Owner`, `Balance`, `Operation`, ...) from the shared schemas instead of hand-written interfaces.
- Keeping it true (`tests/api-contract.test.ts`): the list must equal the routes Express really has (both ways); one scenario calls every operation and parses each real response with its schema; the OpenAPI document must contain every operation. Adding a route without an entry, or changing a response without its schema, fails the tests.
- Limits: schemas are non-strict (an extra field in a response is not caught, a missing or wrongly typed one is). Free-form for now: track explanation, payout snapshot, loaded Believe file, contract, writer mapping result (marked in the document). `errorBody` is used as is, not registered as a named component, because the library patches only its own copy of zod. Schemas are not used to validate responses at run time.
- Consequences: a new route needs its entry in `operations.ts` and, if its body is new, a schema in `api-responses.ts`. Pages that used the removed hand-written types compile unchanged.
- Checked: typecheck, eslint, client build, 186 tests.
- References: D-118
- Status: accepted

## D-120: Agent guides in `docs/agents/`, nested CLAUDE.md files, corrections of stale instructions
- Date: 2026-11-01
- Question: the user asked what an AI agent (Claude Code) needs to understand the project better and to optimise the repository for future development by agents, with minimal code changes; things decided without an explicit answer must be written down.
- Findings (analysis of README, HANDOVER, CLAUDE.md, `docs/`, the code and `context_data/context/`):
  - No single text states the general idea; it is spread over README §5, `docs/01`…`09` (Russian) and 119 log entries.
  - Russian documents and English code have no shared vocabulary list.
  - The log is append-only, so old statuses stay as written: D-088 (retired by D-110), D-102 ("needs measurement", done in D-111/D-114), D-008 (updated by D-079, D-112) and others look open but are not.
  - Stale instructions: README ("from scratch", "do not use Docker", `client/src/`), HANDOVER (133 tests, D-001…D-101, `sources.sqlite`, demo mode), CLAUDE.md (`npx tsx src/main.ts`, `npm run demo`, D-001…D-101), `docs/00_README.md` (modules "not described", links to missing files), `context_data/context/README.md` (`новая_архитектура/app`).
  - Hidden traps not written anywhere: the SQLite modules have no migrations (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`), so a new column never reaches existing databases; one PostgreSQL/ClickHouse store serves every data directory; the `Number(` allow-list in `rules-coverage.test.ts`; the API contract test requires the scenario to call every operation; without `REQUIRE_STACK=1` stack tests are skipped, not failed; no frontend tests.
- Decision (taken by the agent, no explicit answer from the user; reasons given):
  - New folder `docs/agents/` with English guides: `README.md` (index), `01_big_picture.md` (general idea, money pipeline, modules, state, next steps), `02_glossary.md` (RU ↔ EN ↔ code), `03_decision_index.md` (all D-entries by topic with the effective status, ⚠ for owner review), `04_recipes.md` (typical changes and the guard tests), `05_doc_drift.md` (stale statements, precedence, gaps). English because README §1 requires English for everything agents produce; placed in `docs/` because the user asked for additional documentation and it belongs next to the documents it indexes. They summarise and point; the architecture documents and this log stay the source of truth.
  - Nested `CLAUDE.md` in `packages/backend/src/modules/sources/`, `packages/backend/src/facade/`, `packages/backend/tests/`, `packages/frontend/`: Claude Code loads them only when it works in that folder, so the root file stays short and the folder-specific traps are seen exactly when they matter.
  - Root `CLAUDE.md`: a two-line statement of the general idea at the top, `docs/agents/` first in the reading order, current commands (`REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test`, `packages/backend/src/main.ts`, demo marked outdated), the SQLite-migration and shared-store traps, the ⚠ rule and the out-of-scope list.
  - README: a status note on top; "do not use Docker" limited to the old projects (D-103); `client/src/` → `packages/frontend/src/`; §6 allows keeping `docs/agents/` current. HANDOVER: an update banner on top, the body unchanged. The architecture documents (`docs/00`…`09`, `98`, `99`, ADRs, audit) and `context_data/` are not edited (README §6); their stale points are listed in `05_doc_drift.md`.
  - No code changes. The gaps (SQLite migrations, one store per data directory, frontend tests, the data archive predating D-110) are recorded, not fixed: each changes behaviour and should be a separate task.
  - Convention from now on: every new D-entry also gets a line in `docs/agents/03_decision_index.md`; a stale statement found elsewhere gets a row in `05_doc_drift.md`.
- Checked: typecheck, lint, 186 tests with `REQUIRE_STACK=1` before the change; the change touches only Markdown.
- References: `docs/agents/*`, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `HANDOVER.md`, nested `CLAUDE.md` files
- Status: accepted

## D-121: The modules owners, splits, settlements and publishing move from SQLite to PostgreSQL
- Date: 2026-11-01
- Question: the user asked to study the storage and the use cases of every module, move them from SQLite to PostgreSQL, write the migrations, transfer the data and check data entry through the API, on branch `feature/pg`. The two open questions of the earlier talk (one database with a schema per module or a database per module; real foreign keys between modules or soft references) had no explicit answer; the agent took the defaults it had proposed then.
- Decision (taken by the agent where the user gave no answer; reasons given):
  - **One PostgreSQL database, one schema per module:** `owners`, `splits`, `settlements`, `publishing` (the sources registry `source_files` stays in `public`). Reason: a single connection can then read every module in one snapshot (D-012) and write several modules in one transaction; a database per module would make both impossible.
  - **Soft references between modules:** no foreign key crosses a schema (`artist_id`, `document_id`, `owner_id` of other modules are checked by the facade, as before). Within a module all foreign keys are kept, some added (`documents.owner_id`, merge self-references, deferrable). `boundaries.test.ts` checks that every module has its own schema and that no foreign key crosses schemas.
  - **Migrations** `migrations/postgres/0002_splits.sql`, `0003_owners.sql`, `0004_settlements.sql`, `0005_publishing.sql` (runner of D-105, `npm run migrate:pg`). Precise types: `date`, `timestamptz`, `boolean`, `bigint` cents and pico, `numeric` percents (read back canonical, `'14.34'`), JSON kept as text with `IS JSON` checks (the payout snapshot stays byte for byte), CHECKs for enum-like columns and row rules the services guarantee. Split periods of one ISRC cannot overlap at database level (`EXCLUDE USING gist`, `btree_gist`). The SQLite DDL and `shared/db.ts` are removed, with `better-sqlite3`; the "no SQLite migrations" trap of D-120 is gone.
  - **Async access** (`src/shared/pg-db.ts`, `Db`): repositories ask `db.orm()` at every query, which is the transaction connection inside `db.transaction()` / `db.snapshot()` (AsyncLocalStorage) and the pool otherwise. Every service and facade method that touches storage returns a Promise with the old shape. Express 5 routes await them.
  - **Writes stay serialized:** every write transaction takes one advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock(72011301)`), so the check-then-write rules (split overlap, sum of shares, blocking payouts, returns not above the payout) behave as with synchronous SQLite. A B17 load holds it for its database phase (~5–6 s for 420 000 lines; parsing happens before).
  - **D-012 kept:** `calculate()` reads the sources first, then all modules in one read-only REPEATABLE READ transaction (`db.snapshot`). Lists of several modules (owner card, splits page) read in one snapshot too.
  - **Facade writes that span modules are now one transaction** (before: validated first, written module by module): a split with new artists, a split change, a buyout, merge of owners (owners + publishing + settlements), writer resolution and mapping, publishing terms, operations, payment proof, return, and the payout button (checks, calculation and recorded payout in one transaction; the payment service is called after the commit, as before).
  - **The data directory keeps only original files** (`files/sources`, `files/publishing`) and temporary uploads; the module data live in the database of `.env`. A data set is switched with `DB_DATABASE` (and `CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE`), not with `main.ts <dir>`.
  - **Data transfer:** `scripts/migrate-sqlite-to-pg.ts <dataDir> [--replace] [--only …]` copies the four `<m>.sqlite` files (read-only) in foreign-key order, one transaction per module, and rolls back unless row counts and control sums (percents, cents, pico) are equal.
  - **Lint:** type-aware `@typescript-eslint/no-floating-promises` and `await-thenable` for the backend, so a forgotten `await` is an error.
  - **Tests:** `newFacade(threshold, { paymentService })` is async and empties the module tables of the test file's private PostgreSQL database (which already held the sources, D-109); the module tests run on PostgreSQL, not on `:memory:` SQLite.
- Data transferred: `data/pub` (the most complete set: reference data from the old ERP, publishing names, the four real B17 statements) into the `erp` database: 1 114 owners, 1 107 artists, 17 275 tracks, 361 documents, 341 contracts, 13 610 splits / 24 754 shares, 1 054 229 B17 lines, 46 writers, 98 terms; counts and sums equal. `data/check` (the same reference data plus two stub payouts of a test run) was not transferred; `data/real` is empty in this checkout.
- Checked:
  - Recalculation on the transferred data equals the old SQLite code on a copy of `data/pub` exactly: 721 975 units, 4 295 publishing accruals, all 38 reconciliation rows and the balance (earned, waiting, payable) of all 1 114 owners; the calculation took 6.2 s (6.9 s on SQLite).
  - Every real B17 statement loads into PostgreSQL with the stored line count, total and sha256; the 2 963 aggregated rows equal the SQLite query.
  - Typecheck, lint, `REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test` (186 tests).
  - Data entry through the API on a copy of the transferred data (database `erp_apicheck`: migrations from zero by the runner, applied twice, `data/pub` transferred, the Believe registry copied; dropped afterwards), server `main.ts` on it: owner, artist, track (duplicate ISRC+UPC → 409), contract document approved, contract, appendix, split with a new co-artist (percents read back as entered, our share 29.5), split above 100 % → 422 and its new artist was not created (one transaction), overlapping split → 409, split change from a date (two periods), owner card and balance, deduction and credit (a credit counts only with a basis document, as in the SQLite code), plain payout through operations → 422 (D-051), reconciliation without discrepancy, payout button for a real confirmed owner (snapshot with the 47 Believe files and 4 B17 statements, second payout blocked, payment proof attached, payable down to the sub-cent remainder); everything was there after a server restart.
- Known limits: `PaymentService.send` stays synchronous (stub); unordered lists (`listWriters`, `all*`) come in PostgreSQL heap order (callers that show them sort or do not depend on it); a crashed process leaves no half-written module data (transactions), but the B17 original file copy may stay on disk, as before.
- References: `packages/backend/migrations/postgres/0002…0005`, `src/shared/pg-db.ts`, `src/modules/{owners,splits,settlements,publishing}`, `src/facade/*`, `scripts/migrate-sqlite-to-pg.ts`, `tests/helpers/*`, `tests/boundaries.test.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-122: Step timings behind `LOG_TIMINGS=1`; an aggregate projection `units` in `believe.rows`
- Date: 2026-11-01
- Question: the user asked why the owner balance page is slow, what it reads from ClickHouse and how, then for timing logs per step, then for a way to make ClickHouse faster, and accepted the aggregate projection after it was measured on a copy of the real rows.
- Measured (owner of the demo data copied into the database `erp_demo`, 47 Believe files, 75 744 752 lines): the balance took ~6.0 s. `calculate()` reads all units of all loaded files (D-012): ClickHouse ~2.65 s (the `units` view aggregates every line: 2.78 GiB read on the server, 1.7 s), `parsePico` 0.3 s, the JS allocation of 721 975 units 2.8–3.0 s; PostgreSQL ~0.15 s, the owner's own balance and the JSON answer below 0.1 s.
- Decision:
  - **Timings** (`src/shared/timing.ts`): `timed`, `timedSync`, `timedStep`, `note`. Off unless `LOG_TIMINGS=1`; nested steps are collected per outermost step (AsyncLocalStorage) and printed as one tree when it ends. Wired into `GET /owners/:id/balance`, `balanceWithFiles`, `calculate()` (each module read of the snapshot, the units loop), `sources.snapshot()` and every ClickHouse read of `columnar.ts` (time to the first bytes, read + parse, `query_id` for `system.query_log`). Diagnostics only: no behaviour change.
  - **Aggregate projection** `units` of `believe.rows` (`migrations/clickhouse/0002_units_projection.sql`): sums of quantity, gross and net by file, ISRC, UPC, reporting month and sales month inside every part; `MATERIALIZE PROJECTION … mutations_sync = 1` builds it for the parts already loaded. The `believe.units` view and its query stay as they are: ClickHouse answers them from the projection itself.
  - Why not the alternatives (measured or checked on ClickHouse 26.8): query settings (`optimize_aggregation_in_order`, key order, Int128 sums) give nothing or are slower (in-order reading merges 185 parts into 5 streams). A materialized view is an insert trigger: it does not see `ATTACH PARTITION` (how the loader adds a file, D-108) nor `DROP PARTITION`, checked on test tables. A separate pre-aggregated table is faster (0.03 s) but must be kept in step by hand at every load, replace and delete; the projection is part of each part, so it arrives with `ATTACH` and goes with `DROP`. The stage table is created `AS believe.rows` and inherits it, so a file's aggregate is built while it is written to the stage. Filtering the units by the owner's ISRCs would be faster still but changes D-012 (the payout snapshot relies on the full calculation): not done, a product owner question.
  - Raw lines are untouched and not merged (D-102 kept); the projection is derived data and can be rebuilt (`MATERIALIZE PROJECTION`).
  - `columnar.units()` reads `JSONCompactEachRow` (arrays): 87 MB instead of 108 MB for the real data.
- Checked: on a copy of `believe.rows` (hard-linked partitions in a scratch database, dropped) the view answered from the projection (`system.query_log.projections`), 0.14 s instead of 1.7 s, 979 623 rows (53 MiB) read instead of 75.7 M; the result equals the raw aggregation (`EXCEPT` both ways empty). Migration applied to the local stack in 9 s, all 185 parts carry the projection. The balance of the measured owner: ~4.2 s (ClickHouse 0.82 s, of which 0.14 s on the server); the answer is byte for byte the one of the server before the change. New test in `sources-loader.test.ts`: a loaded file's parts carry `units`, `EXPLAIN` reads `ReadFromMergeTree (units)`, results equal with the projection off. Typecheck, lint, `REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test` (187 tests).
- Known limits: a part without the projection (a stage table created otherwise, a table rebuilt without the migration) still attaches and is counted correctly from the raw lines, only slowly; the test above guards the loader path. The rest of the 4.2 s is JS (JSON parse 0.6 s, `parsePico` 0.3 s, allocation 2.9 s): next candidate, no rule change needed.
- References: `packages/backend/migrations/clickhouse/0002_units_projection.sql`, `src/shared/timing.ts`, `src/modules/sources/columnar.ts`, `src/modules/sources/service.ts`, `src/facade/calculation.ts`, `src/facade/context.ts`, `src/api/routes/owners.ts`, `tests/sources-loader.test.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-123: Layered calculation of the master money: the design of the MVP goal
- Date: 2026-11-02
- Question: the MVP goal (`notes/goal_prompt.md`) asks the ERP to compute every case of `notes/settlement_landscape.md` (MVP list): whose each dollar is and how much an owner can be paid today, with the flat hundred of the split replaced by layers with an explicit base (net, artist pool, label share, artist share) and payer (us, sublabel, label representative). Stage 1 verified the cases (`docs/agents/09_settlement_cases.md`), stage 2 designed the model (`10_target_model.md`, `11_plan.md`, `12_golden_cases.md`); both accepted by a council of three independent reviewers (`docs/agents/reviews/stage1_*.md`, `stage2_*.md`).
- Decision (the design; each later step records its own entry D-124 … D-134 as planned in `11_plan.md`):
  - **Modules compute no money.** A new reference module `labels` (our labels 0to8 and Echo8, imprint aliases, sublabel rules); the `splits` module stores rows and **participations** (percent of a base `net` | `bucket`, funded from `top` | `artist_pool` | `artist_share` | `label_share`, period, document); the publishing module returns lines with `operation_type`, writers and terms; the settlements module keeps operations; the author share, the writer parts and the recoupment algorithm move into the facade as layers.
  - **Every recipient is a pseudonym with an owner.** A company, sublabel, representative, buyer or participant gets its own pseudonym under an owner of kind `label` (or `person`); a participation has its own pseudonym, so its income is its own income item and an advance on it recoups only from it (M19 participation-only, the NoGhost advance).
  - **Layer order, fixed in one place:** label of record → perimeter → off the top → split → carves (pool, artist share, label share, sublabel) → categorise; the publishing chain: writer parts → author share by owner, period and operation type → categorise. Each layer is a pure function with its own test.
  - **Exactness:** every allocation is quantised to 12 decimals after its multiplication, every remainder is obtained by subtraction, pro-rata residues go to the row with the largest absolute amount; A = B + C + ours holds exactly per source, account and month; `ourShare` comes from the layer state so that a lost amount shows as a discrepancy.
  - **New C reasons:** `assumption` (an allocation whose document is of the new kind `assumption`, never approved: a council decision the owner has not confirmed), `rule_unconfirmed` (a participation or label rule without an approved document), `label_unmapped` (a Believe label string outside the dictionary: the rows are paid, "ours" waits).
  - **Decisions taken by the council where no source answers** (the money that depends on an assumption stays in C): Q1 sample letters are a percent of net, the "less other participants" clause is an assumption on the licensor's row; Q5 conflicting documents on one ISRC: the registered split stands, the other rows carry an assumption document; Q11 label-share carves before the sublabel half, both marked assumption when they meet on one unit; Q12 sublabel strings outside owner decision 2 get the half rule under an assumption document; R1 a sublabel on a unit without a split takes half of the whole net, as an assumption; the NoGhost rule runs from its agreement's month 2026-06, the earlier months of the imprint under an assumption; one balance per owner across both our labels, shown per paying label (assumption); a carve larger than its bucket takes the bucket and reports a shortfall (P08 §6.7(a) for the artist pool, an assumption for the label share and over-100 tops).
  - **Decisions with a source:** Q3 a registered split applies from the first report month of the ISRC unless the instrument has a date (the payment fact in `0to8 Main.xlsx`, map §6 exception); Q4 the RioX base is net (contract); Q6 the settlement 15 % is a percent of net paid from the label share (contract); M52 rule dates are floored to the first day of their month, the whole month goes to the new holder (a convention on month granularity; D-025 kept); M33 a lump that is the consideration for past uses is a document only, a lump that is accrued royalty paid at signing is a payout; M29 a derivative outside its authorisation is a note on the track (signal, no money); E15 an unmapped imprint string holds back "ours" only.
  - **Kept as they are:** D-025, D-027, D-029 (refined: no split → the label share is the whole net), D-032, D-035 ⚠, D-043 ⚠, D-045 ⚠, D-049…D-101, D-087 ⚠; D-067 ⚠ is closed by the sublabel rule of this design (owner decision 2 of 2026-11-02).
  - **Out of the MVP:** ads, payment fees, thresholds, currency, payees and representatives, recoup holidays and 100 %-of-gross recoup, inbound money from other labels and platforms, no-ISRC folding, no-split pools, taxes, period locks, an advance scope by UPC (`09` §3, `10` §9).
- Checked: three council reviews per stage, with hand recomputation of every golden case (`12_golden_cases.md`, 44 master cases and 5 publishing cases) and verification of the data facts against ClickHouse, PostgreSQL and Annex 14; test baseline at `e2524f4`: typecheck and lint clean, 187 tests (one flaky, fixed in step 3.1).
- References: `docs/agents/09_settlement_cases.md`, `10_target_model.md`, `11_plan.md`, `12_golden_cases.md`, `docs/agents/reviews/stage1_*.md`, `stage2_*.md`, `notes/goal_prompt.md`, `notes/settlement_landscape.md`
- Status: accepted (design); implementation steps follow as D-124 …

## D-124: The Believe unit carries the label string (projection `units_by_label`)
- Date: 2026-11-02
- Question: step 3.1 of `docs/agents/11_plan.md` (layered calculation, D-123): the label of record is chosen from the `Label Name` string of the report line, so the unit of calculation must keep it. Until now the unit was file + ISRC + UPC + reporting month + sales month and the label string was lost in the sum. Also the D-122 loader test was flaky because `columnar.units()` had no `ORDER BY`.
- Decision:
  - **Migration** `migrations/clickhouse/0003_units_by_label.sql` (0001 and 0002 untouched): drop the projection `units`, add `units_by_label` (the same sums, `GROUP BY file_id, isrc, upc, reporting_month, sales_month, label_name`), `MATERIALIZE PROJECTION … mutations_sync = 1`, `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW believe.units` grouped by `label_name` too and exposing it. All statements idempotent. The loader stage table is still `CREATE TABLE … AS believe.rows`, so a file's parts carry the new projection (test below).
  - **Unit key** is now file + ISRC + UPC + reporting month + sales month + label string. One ISRC/UPC/month with two label strings gives two units, each with its label.
  - `columnar.units()` selects `label_name` (alias `label`, not a column name) and has `ORDER BY isrc, upc, reporting_month, sales_month, label_name, file_id`: the order is deterministic. `SourceUnit.labelName` and `UnitResult.labelName` (carried through, no calculation change yet; the label is used from step 3.4).
  - Tests: the projection test points at `units_by_label`, asserts through `EXPLAIN` that the view reads `ReadFromMergeTree (units_by_label)` and compares results sorted; `clickhouse-migrate.test.ts` checks the shipped migrations (numbering, the four statements of 0003); two units of one ISRC/UPC/month with different labels stay two (loader and facade level); the fixture default `Test Label` reaches `SourceUnit.labelName`. Fixtures: `BelieveRowSpec.labelName` and `Spec.labelName`; `believeFileName` gets a distinct report id per call unless given.
- Checked: migration applied to the local stack (`npm run migrate:ch`, 16 s including start-up). `LOG_TIMINGS=1`, balance of one owner on the real data copy (47 files, 721 975 units, the same count as before: the real files hold one label string per key): `GET /owners/:id/balance` 4.2 s warm, 4.8 s first call (budget 10 s); `calculate` 4.1 s; ClickHouse units read 0.87 s (first byte 0.37 s, read + parse 0.45 s), `parsePico` 0.29 s, JS allocation 1.9 s, `publishing.accruals` 0.93 s. No slowdown against D-122 (4.2 s, 0.82 s). Typecheck, lint, `REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test`: 192 tests.
- References: `packages/backend/migrations/clickhouse/0003_units_by_label.sql`, `src/modules/sources/columnar.ts`, `src/modules/sources/service.ts`, `src/facade/calculation.ts`, `tests/sources-loader.test.ts`, `tests/sources.test.ts`, `tests/clickhouse-migrate.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/fixtures.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-125: Labels module (own labels, aliases, sublabel rules); tracks.note; document kind assumption
- Date: 2026-11-02
- Question: step 3.2 of the layered-calculation plan (D-123): where do our labels, the imprint strings of Believe and the sublabel rule live, and what do owners need for the notes and assumption documents of the design?
- Decision:
  - **New module `labels`** (schema `labels`, `migrations/postgres/0006_labels.sql`, `modules/labels/{schema,repository,service,index}.ts`, `CLAUDE.md`): `labels` (`own` or `sublabel`; a sublabel has `own_label_id`, an own label none, enforced by CHECK), `label_aliases` (key = normalised string: NFKC, lower case, spaces collapsed), `label_rules` (percent of the label share, recipient pseudonym, `[valid_from, valid_to)`, document, an exclusion constraint: one rule per label and date). No foreign key to other modules; the facade checks artist and document ids.
  - Service: `createLabel`, `addAlias` (duplicate → 409 `alias_taken`), `setRule` (sublabels only, `rule_for_own_label`; overlap → 409 `rule_overlap`; percent like a split share), `closeRule`, `getLabel`, `listLabels`, `index()` with `labelOf(string, date)` (label, own label, rule in force or null; unknown string → undefined) and `normalize`. No money is computed.
  - **Owners**: `owners.tracks.note` (`0007_owners_track_note_assumption.sql`; `createTrack({ note })`, `setTrackNote`, `TrackView.note`, `trackSchema.note`); document kind `assumption` (CHECK widened, `DOCUMENT_KINDS`); `approveDocument` refuses it (422 `assumption_not_approvable`).
  - **Facade**: opens the module, `modules.labels`, passed through the context; no new facade methods yet (3.4, 3.6).
  - **Tests**: `newFacade` seeds own labels 0to8 (aliases `Test Label`, `0to8`) and Echo8 (alias `Echo8`); helpers `labelOwner`, `unconfirmedOwner`, `assumptionDoc`, `approvedDoc` of any kind; `tests/labels.test.ts`; the boundaries test and `resetModules` know the schema.
- Checked: typecheck, lint, `REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test` (193 tests).
- Known limits: dates are not floored to month start here (the facade does it, M52, step 3.3); no API routes yet (3.6).
- References: `packages/backend/migrations/postgres/0006_labels.sql`, `0007_owners_track_note_assumption.sql`, `src/modules/labels`, `src/modules/owners`, `src/facade/{index,context}.ts`, `tests/labels.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/fixtures.ts`, `docs/agents/10_target_model.md`
- Status: accepted

## D-126: Participations in the splits module; per-row documents; the row-or-participation rule; rule dates floored to the month start
- Date: 2026-11-02
- Question: step 3.3 of the plan (`docs/agents/11_plan.md`, target model `10_target_model.md` §1–§2): where do participations (remixer, MC from the pool, A&R, settlement, beat) live, how does a split row carry its own document, and how are mid-month rule dates entered.
- Decision:
  - **Storage** (`migrations/postgres/0008_participations.sql`, `schema.ts`): `splits.split_shares.document_id` (a row's own document overrides the split's for that row); table `splits.participations` (`isrc`, `artist_id`, `percent` numeric(9,6), `base` net|bucket, `funded_from` top|artist_pool|artist_share|label_share, `from_artist_id`, `valid_from`, `valid_to` exclusive and > valid_from, `document_id`, `origin`, `note`). CHECKs: `top`/`artist_pool` need `base = net`; `artist_share` needs `bucket` and `from_artist_id` different from the recipient; `label_share` has no `from_artist_id`. Exclusion constraint: no overlap for one (isrc, artist_id, funded_from).
  - **Service**: `createParticipation` (one write transaction): overlap → `ConflictError participation_overlap`; the recipient is a split row (percent > 0) on that ISRC in a split overlapping the period → `RuleViolationError double_participation`; for `top` the sum of overlapping `top` percents plus this one above 100 → `top_above_100`. `closeParticipation(id, validTo)` (validTo after valid_from; it may only shorten), `listParticipations`. `createSplit`/`changeSplit` refuse a row with percent > 0 for an artist that has a participation overlapping the new split's period (`double_participation`: the rule holds in both directions; a 0 row, as a buyout writes, is allowed). `index()` gains `participationsAt(isrc, date)` (order top, artist_pool, artist_share, label_share, then validFrom, id); `at()` shares carry `documentId`; `documentIds()` includes row and participation documents.
  - **Facade** (M52): `createSplit.validFrom`, `changeSplit.from`, `buyout.effectiveFrom` and `createParticipation.validFrom` are floored to the first day of the month (`monthStart`); `validTo` is not. Facade `createParticipation`, `closeParticipation`, `listParticipations` (pseudonyms, owner ids, document status). `checkSplitDocument` now also checks each row's document and a participation's document; allowed kinds appendix, contract, buyout, other, assumption (`assumption` is only a string in the list until step 3.2 adds the kind).
  - **Shared schemas**: split share items accept optional `documentId`; response shares carry `documentId` (nullable). No participation routes yet (step 3.6). `calculation.ts` is not changed (step 3.4 reads the participations).
- Checked: new `tests/participations.test.ts` (9 tests: every kind, invalid combinations, overlap, G23 both directions and `top_above_100`, close, `participationsAt` order, row documents round trip, G25 storage half and flooring of split, change and buyout). Typecheck, lint, `REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test`.
- Known limits: the double-participation check compares ids as stored (a merged artist id is canonicalised only at facade entry); `top_above_100` sums every overlapping `top` participation of the ISRC, even if two of them never overlap each other within the period.
- References: `packages/backend/migrations/postgres/0008_participations.sql`, `src/modules/splits/*`, `src/facade/{splits,context}.ts`, `packages/shared/src/api-{schemas,responses}.ts`, `tests/participations.test.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-128: Publishing terms by operation type (part 1: module)
- Date: 2026-11-02
- Question: step 3.5 of `docs/agents/11_plan.md` (layered calculation, D-123): the author's percentage may differ by the `operation type` of a B17 line (golden cases G26a–e). The module has to keep the type per line group and per term. The author share itself moves into the facade in part 2.
- Decision:
  - **Migration** `migrations/postgres/0009_pub_terms_operation_type.sql` (0005 untouched, idempotent): `publishing.pub_terms.operation_type text` (NULL = for any type) and a CHECK that a given type is not empty. `0005` had no overlap constraint (the service checks it), so none was added: overlap and closing are checked in the service only, per (owner, operation type).
  - `aggregatedLines()` groups also by `operation_type` and returns it (`AggregatedLine.operationType`); `ORDER BY` has `operation_type COLLATE "C" NULLS FIRST` last, so the order stays deterministic.
  - `setTerms({ ownerId, percent, validFrom, documentId?, operationType? })`: a term must start after every term of the same (owner, type); a new term closes the open term of its own type and then needs a document; a typed and an untyped term may coexist for one owner and period. An empty type is a validation error. `termsOf`, `allTerms` and the rows carry `operationType`.
  - `accruals()` (still in the module until part 2): for a line it takes the term with the line's operation type first, then the term without a type, then the default 50 %; `PublishingAccrual.operationType` carries the type.
  - **Facade and API shapes:** `setPublishingTerms` passes `operationType` through; `publishingTermsBody` gets optional `operationType` (non-empty), `publishingTermsSchema` gets `operationType` (nullable). Fixture `writeB17`: `B17RowSpec.operationType` (default `Performance`).
  - Module notes: `src/modules/publishing/CLAUDE.md`.
- Checked: typecheck, lint, `REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test` (212 tests; `tests/publishing-terms.test.ts` new: typed term wins and untyped covers the rest, typed term alone with default for other types, overlap per type, closing per type with the document rule, empty type refused). Migration applied to the local stack.
- Known limits: part 2 (author share in the facade layers, `layers/writer-parts.ts`, `layers/author-share.ts`) follows; the owner page does not show the type yet.
- References: `packages/backend/migrations/postgres/0009_pub_terms_operation_type.sql`, `src/modules/publishing/*`, `src/facade/sources.ts`, `packages/shared/src/api-schemas.ts`, `api-responses.ts`, `tests/publishing-terms.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/fixtures.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-127: Facade layers for the master money; reconciliation per account with ours per label; new C reasons; recoup moved to the facade
- Date: 2026-11-02
- Question: steps 3.4a and 3.4b of `docs/agents/11_plan.md` (layered calculation, D-123): the master money must be computed by pure layers in a fixed order, as specified in `docs/agents/10_target_model.md` §3, every golden case of `12_golden_cases.md` (G01–G25, G27–G44) must hold end to end, the completeness check must run per Believe account and take our share from the layer state, and the recoupment algorithm moves out of the settlements module.
- Decision:
  - **Pure layers** in `src/facade/layers/` (no storage access; a plain `LayerContext` of functions and maps built by `calculate()` from one snapshot): `label-of-record.ts` → `perimeter.ts` → `off-the-top.ts` → `split.ts` → `carve.ts` → `categorise.ts`. The order is written once, in `layers/index.ts` (`MASTER_LAYERS`); `allocateUnit(unit, ctx, layers?)` folds it and returns the final state and the state after every layer (the perimeter ends the chain). State: unit, `ourLabelId`/`ourLabelName`, sublabel `rule`, `status`, `scaledNet`, `rows` (allocations with `layer`, `kind`, `ruleId`, `documentId`, `percent`, `amount`, `category`, `reason`, `assumption`), `labelShare`, `labelShareCategory` (`ours` | `label_unmapped` | `no_isrc` | `unknown_pair`: the perimeter's C is the label share with its reason, so Σ allocations + label share = net holds for every unit), `shortfalls`.
  - **Rules** (10 §3 exactly): an unmapped label string has no label of record, its label share waits in C `label_unmapped`, rows and carves still run, no sublabel; tops `q(net × p/100)`, scaled net by subtraction, Σp > 100 in data scaled down to the net with the excess as a shortfall `top_above_100`; split rows on the scaled net, percent 0 skipped, no split → `no_split`; carves in one function, four kinds in a fixed order (pool, artist share, label share, sublabel), one shared base per kind: pool targets taken from the rows pro rata to their amounts at the start of the kind; artist-share carves from the source row (missing row → 0 and a shortfall `no_source_row`; carves above 100 % of one row scaled down to it, the architect's R2-2); label-share carves from the label share at the start of the kind (base `net` or `bucket`), scaled down if above it; sublabel `q(labelShare × rule.percent/100)`. A carve larger than its bucket takes the bucket and records a shortfall `bucket_short`; no money is created. An allocation is emitted whenever its percent is non-zero, even with amount 0.
  - **Exactness:** every amount is quantised to 12 decimals half up right after its multiplication (`layers/exact.ts`: `q`, `pct`); every remainder is a subtraction; pro rata and scale-down shares are quantised with the residue to the item with the largest absolute amount (a tie: the first by artist id). So Σ allocations + label share = net exactly after every layer, independent of decimal.js precision.
  - **Categorise** (first reason wins): the allocation's document is of kind `assumption` (a row: its own document, else the split's), or Q11 (a unit with a label-share carve and a sublabel allocation marks both), or R1 (a sublabel on a `no_split` unit) → `assumption` (the basis is kept: `document` | `q11` | `r1`); document not approved → `split_unconfirmed` (rows) / `rule_unconfirmed` (tops, carves, sublabel); no owner → `artist_without_owner`; owner not confirmed → `owner_unconfirmed`; else B. Module 1 gives the kind with the status (`owners.documentInfo`).
  - **`calculate()`** reads the labels index (now with `labelName(id)` and `documentIds()`) and the participations inside the same `db.snapshot`, runs `allocateUnit` per unit; the old unit loop is deleted. `UnitResult` keeps its fields and adds `ourLabelId`, `ourLabelName`, `labelShare`, `labelShareCategory`, `shortfalls` (`ours` = the label share when it is ours, else 0); `calculate(m, docs, { layers, traceIsrc })` takes a layer list (the broken-layer test) and keeps the per-layer states only for a traced ISRC.
  - **`reconcile()`** rows by source, **account** and month (`account`; B17 rows have account `b17`), file totals per account; `ourShare` = Σ of the units' label share where it is ours (no longer A − B − C), `ourShareByLabel` (our label name → amount), `C_breakdown` with `assumption`, `rule_unconfirmed`, `label_unmapped`, `ourShareNoSplit` = the ours remainder of `no_split` units; `discrepancy = A − B − C − ourShare` must be 0 (a test with a deliberately broken split layer shows 20).
  - **`signals()`** adds `unfunded` (shortfalls), `labelUnmapped` (string → net), `tracksWithNote`, `participantsWithoutOwner` (with the amount in the period), `assumptions` (document id and basis → amount, ISRCs). **`explainTrack()`** returns the participations and per unit the state after every layer.
  - **Shared schemas:** the C reasons are one exported enum `C_REASONS` / `cReasonSchema` (`api-responses.ts`), used by the reconciliation and balance breakdowns (`z.partialRecord`); reconciliation rows have `account` and `ourShareByLabel`; the new signal lists; `explainSchema` replaces the free-form explanation in `operations.ts`. No new routes.
  - **Recoupment** moves as is to `src/facade/layers/recoup.ts`; the shapes `Scope`, `ScopeKind`, `IncomeItem`, `Position`, `RecoupResult` stay exported by the settlements module (`recoup-types.ts`), so operations keep their scope validation there. `balance.ts` changed only its imports.
  - **Splits fix:** a buyout copies the other rows' own documents into the new split (before, a row under an `assumption` document lost it at a buyout; G10b).
- Checked: `tests/layers/*.test.ts` (62 pure tests, no stack: one file per layer with the layer half of the golden cases, the seeded random exactness test over 10,000 units with 6-decimal percents in every position, nets up to 1e5 with 12 decimals, every kind of carve, signs and zeros, asserting Σ allocations + label share = net and at most 12 decimals after every layer, the shuffled-order test, the pure over-100 top cap); `tests/golden.test.ts` (52 tests: every master case G01–G25, G27–G44 end to end with the exact decimal strings, each also checking the reconciliation identity, discrepancy 0, explain summing to the unit, owner balances; the broken-layer discrepancy test). Typecheck and lint clean; `REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test`: 326 tests, 325 pass, 1 guard assertion red (below). `LOG_TIMINGS=1`, real data in the dev database (47 files, 721 975 units; no labels loaded yet, so every unit is `label_unmapped`), `GET /owners/:id/balance` of the owner with the most split rows: 4.8–5.2 s warm (budget 10 s); `calculate` 5.0 s: ClickHouse units 0.95 s, `parsePico` 0.3 s, the layers 2.4 s (the old loop 1.9 s, D-124), `publishing.accruals` 1.1 s.
- Known limits: `tests/rules-coverage.test.ts` "balance, reconciliation and payout snapshot carry money as decimal strings or whole cents only" expects the unquantised product `333.43332999900033333333` (1000.000000000001 × 33.333333 %); with the 12-decimal rule of D-123 the earned amount is `333.433329999`. The guard was not changed (the task forbids weakening a guard): it waits for the orchestrator / owner to update the expectation. On the dev data every unit is `label_unmapped` until the labels dictionary is loaded (step 4.1), so its reconciliation shows "ours" in C. This entry is appended after D-128 (part 1 landed first).
- References: `packages/backend/src/facade/layers/*`, `src/facade/{calculation,reports,balance}.ts`, `src/modules/settlements/recoup-types.ts`, `src/modules/labels/service.ts`, `src/modules/owners/service.ts`, `src/modules/splits/service.ts`, `src/api/operations.ts`, `packages/shared/src/api-responses.ts`, `tests/layers/*`, `tests/golden.test.ts`, `tests/recoup.test.ts`, `docs/agents/10_target_model.md`, `12_golden_cases.md`
- Guard test adjusted (orchestrator, 2026-11-03): `tests/rules-coverage.test.ts` "money as decimal strings or whole cents" expected the unrounded product `333.43332999900033333333`; under the 12-decimal rule of D-123 the earned amount is `333.433329999`, so the expected string was changed to that value; the rule the test guards (no floats, decimal strings only) is unchanged.
- Status: accepted

## D-129: API and pages for labels, participations, terms by type, track notes
- Date: 2026-11-03
- Question: step 3.6 of `docs/agents/11_plan.md`: the labels (D-125), participations (D-126), terms by operation type (D-128), track notes and `assumption` documents (D-125, D-127) exist in the facade but not in the API or on the pages.
- Decision:
  - **Routes** (all in `operations.ts` with schemas): `GET /labels` (labels with aliases and rules; a rule carries `recipientPseudonym` and `documentStatus`), `POST /labels` (`{ name, kind: own|sublabel, ownLabelId? }`), `POST /labels/:id/aliases` (409 `alias_taken`), `POST /labels/:id/rules` (facade `createLabelRule`: start floored to the month start, recipient artist must exist, document kind as for splits), `POST /labels/rules/:id/close` (`{ validTo }`); `GET /participations?isrc=&artistId=` (with pseudonyms and document status), `POST /participations` (facade `createParticipation`), `POST /participations/:id/close`. New routers `routes/labels.ts`, `routes/participations.ts`; the facade got `listLabels` and `createLabelRule` (`facade/splits.ts`).
  - `PATCH /tracks/:id` takes `isrc`+`upc` (together) and/or `note` (null or empty clears it); `createDocumentBody.kind` accepts `assumption` (approving it stays 422 `assumption_not_approvable`); split share rows already take `documentId` and `POST /publishing/terms` already takes `operationType` (checked, now also in the summaries).
  - **Reasons:** `packages/shared/src/reasons.ts` (`@erp/shared/reasons`) holds `REASON_LABELS: Record<CReason, string>`; the type makes a missing label a compile error, `tests/api-reasons.test.ts` checks it at run time and that the reconciliation and balance breakdowns accept only the enum keys. The pages use `reasonLabel()` from `components/ui.tsx`.
  - **Pages:** `/labels` (labels, aliases, rules; forms: create label, add alias, add rule, close rule), `/participations` (ISRC filter, table, create form, close), optional per-row document on the splits page, operation type input on the publishing terms form of the owner page (empty = all types), reasons with labels on the balance and sources pages, `account` and `ourShareByLabel` on the reconciliation table, the lists unfunded, labelUnmapped, tracksWithNote, participantsWithoutOwner, assumptions on the sources page. The track note has an API call (`api.setTrackNote`) but no form yet.
- Checked: `tests/api-contract.test.ts` calls every new operation (label, alias, rule, close; participation create, list, close; track note; assumption document; typed term; a split row with an assumption document) and parses each response; `tests/api.test.ts` (alias taken 409, unknown recipient 404, bad participation 400, assumption approve 422, empty track patch 400); `tests/api-reasons.test.ts`. Typecheck, `npm run build:client`.
- Known limits: error statuses are those of the modules (a bad participation is 400 `validation_error`, not 422); no form for the track note; frontend not opened in a browser.
- References: `packages/backend/src/api/{operations.ts,routes/labels.ts,routes/participations.ts}`, `packages/shared/src/{api-schemas,api-responses,reasons}.ts`, `packages/frontend/src/{labels,splits,sources,owners}/*`, `tests/api-reasons.test.ts`
- Status: accepted

## D-128 (part 2): The author share and the writer parts computed in the facade layers
- Date: 2026-11-03
- Question: step 3.5 part 2 of `docs/agents/11_plan.md`: after the terms by operation type (D-128 part 1) the publishing module still applied the author share inside `accruals()`; the money must be computed in the facade by pure layers (10 §4), like the master money (D-127).
- Decision:
  - **Pure layers** in `src/facade/layers/`: `writer-parts.ts` (an aggregated line → parts per our writer, D-087: equal parts cut at 20 decimals ROUND_DOWN, the last takes the remainder; an ambiguous writer keeps a part with owner null; no writer of ours → the rights-holder), `author-share.ts` (terms of the part's owner at the quarter-end month, `valid_to` exclusive, merged owners resolved; a term with the line's operation type first, else the untyped one; `authorAmount = part × percent / 100` with the arithmetic the module used, no new rounding; no terms → default 50 and `defaultTerms`), `publishing-categorise.ts` (no owner or a label owner → C `writer_unmapped` with author and label amounts 0; owner not confirmed → `owner_unconfirmed`; default or unapproved terms → `publishing_terms_unconfirmed`; else B; `labelAmount = part − authorAmount`). The order is `PUBLISHING_LAYERS` in `layers/index.ts`; `allocateLine(line, ctx)` folds it and returns the parts and the state after every layer. The pure `PublishingContext`: writers map, terms, `resolveOwner`, `labelOwners`, `confirmedOwners`, `documentStatus`.
  - **The module computes no money:** `accruals()` and `PublishingAccrual` are removed; `snapshotForCalculation()` returns `{ writers, terms, lines }` read in one `repo.snapshot` (`aggregatedLines` unchanged). `calculate()` reads it inside its own `db.snapshot`, builds the context (memoised owner resolution) and runs `allocateLine` per line. The `PublishingResult` shape and the reconciliation rows (`source: 'b17'`, account `b17`) are unchanged; results give the same numbers as before.
  - `parseSongwriters` is exported from the publishing module index for the layer.
- Checked: `tests/layers/publishing.test.ts` (pure halves of G26a–d, three-way equal parts, ambiguous writer, holder fallback, label owner, unconfirmed owner and terms, terms by month and merge); `tests/golden.test.ts` G26a–e end to end (A 400, B 170, C 50, ours 180, discrepancy 0); `tests/publishing-terms.test.ts` now reads the percents from `calculate()`. Typecheck, lint, `REQUIRE_STACK=1 npm test`: 346 tests pass.
- Known limits: the typed-term id and the `defaultTerms` flag are no longer exposed outside the layer state (`PublishingResult` is unchanged); the `percent` and `reason` carry what the tests need.
- References: `packages/backend/src/facade/layers/{writer-parts,author-share,publishing-categorise,index}.ts`, `src/facade/calculation.ts`, `src/modules/publishing/{service,index}.ts`, `tests/layers/publishing.test.ts`, `tests/golden.test.ts`
